# Janus snooping ledger

> **No backtest number exists outside this file.** If a result is not attached to a row here, it
> is not a result — it is a scratch calculation and must not be quoted, promoted, or carried into
> a decision.

## The rules

1. **One test look = one new numbered row.** A row is appended with `verdict = PENDING`
   **before** the run, and filled in after. Never the other way round.
2. **BH-FDR threshold = `0.1 / m`**, where `m` = the total number of rows in this ledger at the
   time of the look, *including* the row being spent. So row 1 is judged at 0.1, row 2 at 0.05,
   row 5 at 0.02, and so on. Getting stricter as you look more is the entire point.
3. **A dev-selection grid counts as ONE look** — at the selected config, not at each cell.
4. **Forward-eval rows do not count** as test looks and are recorded as `F#` rows below.
5. **Descriptive characterization rows do not count** and are recorded as `L#` rows.
6. **Rows are immutable.** Correct a row with a *new* row or an appended note, never in place.
7. **Criteria live in the `spec.yaml`** referenced by the row, and are immutable once written.

## Budget state

| | |
|---|---|
| Test looks spent | **2** |
| Numbered rows | **3** (rows 1–2 resolved; row 3 pending) |
| Next test row | **4** after row 3 resolves (row 3 threshold 0.1/3 = 0.0333) |
| Inherits from Kilos | **No** — fresh budget |

**Janus does not inherit Kilos' row count.** Reusing spent Kilos evidence as a Janus
*characterization* or *prior* is free and encouraged — it is already-paid-for knowledge. Using it
as a Janus *test look* is not, and would be double-dipping the same data.

| F1 | 2026-08-20 | Kalshi-open vs PM established price (MLB moneyline) | forward, from 2026-08-20 | **PENDING — shadow trading, rule PINNED before the first decision. Costs NO test look** (AGENTS.md: *"Forward eval rows do not count"*) **for exactly as long as the rule is not tuned** — adjusting any threshold, gate or size after seeing results converts this to dev selection and spends a look. **Hypothesis**: PM lists an MLB game ~82 h before Kalshi opens it (measured +81.9h…+83.4h on 7 games, PM created in a 13:00 UTC batch), so at Kalshi's open PM carries an established price (median 0.085 off its 0.500 listing default) and Kalshi does not. **Rule**: at first sight of a new KXMLBGAME ticker, take EXECUTABLE quotes on both legs; PM must be two-sided, ≥0.02 off 0.500, spread ≤0.10; require net edge after PER-MARKET fees; size $100 capped at binding touch depth; hold to settlement. **BOTH directions pinned** — the inherited mirror was spent and FAILED (−2.375¢, p=0.772), so testing one arm only would be a free parameter. **n counts GAMES, not outcome rows** (94 outcomes = 47 games). **t0 is DISCOVERED, not scheduled**: measured 2026-08-20, Kalshi exposes no MLB market before it opens (0 with future open_time, 0 in any pre-open status), so detection latency = the 60 s poll and `lag_s` is recorded on every row. Detection validated 94/94 against known-good pairs via `lib/entities` (Kalshi ATH/AZ vs PM oak/ari broke a naive parser on 12 of 94). **10-event checkpoint gates on GAP EXISTENCE, not PnL**: binary settlement has ~50¢ SD so n=10 gives ~16¢ standard error and cannot detect a 4¢ edge; going live on 10 events means going live on a demonstrated gap and an unproven edge. **[APPENDED 2026-08-20T22:05Z — FIRST RUN DISCARDED, cold-start contamination. No rule was changed.]** The opening poll had no cold-start guard, so it treated all 94 already-open KXMLBGAME markets as newly discovered and emitted 94 decisions with **lag_s of 1.6 to 71.1 hours** — including **18 TRADE signals at 43–71 h**. Every one is an artifact: the spec defines t0 as *"first observation of the Kalshi market after it opens"*, and a market first seen 71 h after opening was not observed at open. **This is a collector defect, not a parameter tune** — excluding those rows ENFORCES the registered definition rather than altering it, so the row remains a forward eval and still costs no test look. DB preserved as `CONTAMINATED_coldstart_20260820T220341Z.sqlite`; **none of it counts and none of it is scored**. Three guards added and pinned by `tests/test_shadow.py` (147 suite green): a **COLD_START_SEED** pass that marks everything already open without deciding (verified: 94 seeded, 0 decided), a **MAX_LAG_S=900 s / STALE_OPEN** reason so a late-detected open is recorded rather than scored, and a per-decision flush so a mid-batch crash cannot lose observations. Detection validated 94/94 via `lib/entities`. Clock restarted from a clean DB at 22:04:58Z. | `research/exec/2026-08-21_shadow-kalshi-open/spec.yaml` |
| F2 | 2026-08-21 | Kalshi↔PM convergence attribution (MLB moneyline) | forward, from 2026-08-21 | **PENDING — WHO MOVES? Registered BEFORE the first sample was read. Forward eval, costs NO test look** while the statistic stays pinned. **Motivated by F1's first observation**: Kalshi opened ATL@MIL at **0.25/0.95 — a 70¢ spread** — so the two-leg round trip cost **143.9¢ for a 100¢ payoff**. Arbitrage is dead. A ONE-LEGGED convergence bet pays only one spread, giving an exact condition: **edge = |mid_error| − half_spread**, i.e. **spread < 2 × |error|**. At that open: error 6.5¢, half-spread 35¢. Five hours later the same market quoted **0.50/0.58** — spread collapsed to 8¢. So the question is a RACE: does the spread fall below 2× the error before the error decays? **Statistic is ATTRIBUTION, not correlation**: decompose d_error into the Kalshi move and the PM move and report each venue's share of the closure — two series can correlate with either one leading. **Answers fast because convergence needs NO SETTLEMENT**: an observation is one poll, not one settled game, so the ~50¢ binary variance that makes n=10 useless for PnL never enters; both venues batch top-of-book (all 90 open MLB markets in ONE Kalshi call) so 90 markets are sampled every 60 s. Mids are used for convergence ONLY; every tradeability claim uses executable prices and the pinned `tradeable` flag — mid-price scoring produced phase 1's 47¢ ghost and L21's 24 fictional arbitrages. **Direction is genuinely open**: AGENTS.md's inherited *"truth sits at Kalshi"* was measured on crypto where Kalshi LEADS, and here Kalshi is the late lister by ~82 h. **No outcome is ever read.** | `research/exec/2026-08-21_convergence/spec.yaml` |

Mechanism identified and measured: the BRTI−Chainlink index difference (median **1.28 bps**) equals the reference wedge (1.28 bps); disagreement is a small-move effect (median \|move\| 9.70 bps when they agree vs **1.03 bps** when they don't); P(disagree \| close between the two references) = **42.71%** vs **3.37%** outside. It says nothing about whether prices diverge, both sides are tradeable, or depth exists (filters 2–4, which killed more candidates than cost did). (c) Kalshi's per-order ceil is a **1.14–1.59× tax at n=1**, flat 1¢ outside p∈[0.16,0.84] and 2¢ inside → any strategy selecting on extreme prices inherits a cost advantage with zero informational content; carried to Track B as a mandatory flat-2¢ control. A corrected labeller was applied UNIFORMLY to all 128 rows, produced EXACTLY ONE change (0→1) and ZERO changes that would have hurt precision, and still refuses to cross-match Cubs↔White Sox. A slug/ticker-date join would have dropped precisely the **postponed** games — the highest-settlement-wedge subset — an invisible bias pointing the wrong way. Using `markets[].gameStartTime` (PM) and the rules text (Kalshi), the two agree EXACTLY. (c) **A false alias was auto-derived and caught**: `nfl "la" → Rams (n=4)`, unambiguous only because the Chargers hadn't appeared with that label yet. Now barred by test — a ≤3-char code is trusted only when its team's location is unique in the league. The six real ambiguities (`mlb/chicago`, `mlb|nfl|nhl/new york`, `mlb|nfl/los angeles`) all **refuse to resolve**, which matters because Kalshi renders MLB teams by LOCATION ONLY. | | | | **[APPENDED 2026-08-19T19:35Z — Phase A was SILENTLY FAILING its registered gate; root cause found and fixed; clock restarted.]** The report read `PENDING_INSUFFICIENT_DURATION`, which looked like "needs more time". It was not. At 50.8 h the capture rate was **31.3%** against the registered **>=98%** gate, no series exceeded 46.7%, and the missingness was **strongly non-random in the worst possible direction**: capture at **t=0 was 11.3%**, 0.5 s 17.1%, 1 s 23.2% — i.e. worst exactly at the offsets where the opening-collapse thesis lives — and by volatility bucket Q1 5.2% / Q2 19.5% / Q3 19.9% / Q4 18.0%, with 3,426 windows unbucketable for want of a reference price. That is Trap 2 realized. **Root cause (M1): the collector ran as launchd `ProcessType Background` + `Nice 19` + `LowPriorityIO`, pinning it at runtime priority 4** — the IDENTICAL pathology that made `com.janus.ibkr-gateway` unusable (priority 4 -> paged-out JVM -> 10 s TLS timeouts), and it cannot hit t=0/0.5/1/2/3/5 s sampling targets there. Fixed the same way: `ProcessType Interactive`, `Nice`/`LowPriorityIO` removed; runtime priority now **37**. **Result after the fix: `scheduler_late` 19,843 -> 0, and 19 series at 100.0% capture** (12 Kalshi + 7 PM), t=0 11.3% -> 63.2% and uniform across every offset. The residual 36.8% is entirely `market_not_discovered` from **7 series that do not exist** (KXADA15M, KXBCH15M, KXCRYPTOCOMP15M, KXCRYPTOLEAD15M, KXINX15M, KXNDQ15M, KXTON15M — 0/2,520 each pre-fix); that is a SCOPING question about the registered ">=12 series" denominator, not a capture failure. **Clock restarted**: the 50.8 h of contaminated data is PRESERVED, not deleted, as `episodes_preQoSfix_2026-08-19T19Z.sqlite` (1.17 GB); a fresh DB began 2026-08-19T19:04Z. Post-fix figures are an EARLY read (~11 resolved rows/series, about one window cycle) — indicative, not the gate. Gate still requires 72 h. No test look spent; no outcome or PnL read. | `research/exec/2026-08-17_track-b-phase-a/spec.yaml` |

| L13 | 2026-08-18 | exec / Track B | **PENDING — IBKR live-account read-only capability + entitlement probe.** Fail-closed loopback transport first; no order/trade/account-management surface. | L25 | 2026-08-20 | exec / cross-venue | **PENDING — does EDGE live where SIZE lives? THE PROJECT'S FIRST TEST LOOK (test_looks_spent: 1), operator-authorised.** Registered BEFORE the statistic was computed. Motivated by the 2026-08-20 capacity characterization: the top decile of markets holds **46.4%** of all transactable dollars (top 50 = 76.2% of 509 unique markets), so an edge that lives only in the thin tail cannot be traded at scale. **Unit is one UNIQUE KALSHI MARKET, never one pair** — summing per pair triple-counted capacity earlier the same day ($131,099 vs the correct $38,918). `capacity_usd` = min(Kalshi touch, PM touch), the BINDING leg. `edge_cents` = `screen.py` nominal edge at **EXECUTABLE prices with PER-MARKET fees**, max across a market's pairs; mid-price scoring is forbidden (phase 1's 47¢ ghost; L21's 24 fictional arbs). **Primary**: median(edge | top capacity decile) − median(edge | rest), two-sided permutation, 10,000 shuffles, seed 0. **Secondary**: Spearman rho(capacity, edge), same framework. **Significance p < 0.004** (BH-FDR q=0.10, m=25). **Robustness PRE-COMMITTED and reported regardless**: repeat on |edge| ≤ 20¢, because false pairs manufacture huge apparent edges and are expected to concentrate in THIN markets — a confound that would manufacture exactly the negative correlation being tested for. **PRIOR-EXPOSURE DISCLOSED**: the nominal-edge distribution on this same corpus was observed earlier the same session (p50 +5.4¢, 1,046 pairs ≥2¢, top hits inspected and found to be artifacts), so this is NOT a virgin look at the edge variable; no criterion was chosen with reference to it, and the hypothesis is a microstructure argument, but a reader must be able to discount it. **Reads NO outcome and NO settlement** — it can establish only that edge is or is not anti-correlated with size, which is a NECESSARY condition for the trade and never a sufficient one. **Largest threat, stated before the run**: `match/gold/nonsports.csv` is UNLABELLED so verifier precision is unmeasured and the false-pair share is unknown. | `research/exec/2026-08-20_capacity-edge-correlation/spec.yaml` |
| | | | **[APPENDED 2026-08-20 — EXECUTED. Verdict `NOT_SIGNIFICANT`. The first test look is SPENT.]** Population 509 unique Kalshi markets, 494 scored (15 excluded `pm_one_sided`, counted not dropped). Bar p<0.0040 (BH-FDR q=0.10, m=25). **Primary**: median edge top capacity decile **43.758¢** vs rest **24.471¢**, diff **+19.287¢**, **p=0.128** — NOT significant. **Secondary**: Spearman **rho=−0.0646, p=0.154** — NOT significant. **Robustness |edge|≤20¢** (pre-committed): n=193, 2.358¢ vs 0.299¢, diff +2.059¢, p=0.365; rho=−0.1128, p=0.120 — NOT significant. Per the registered rule this is the `not_significant` branch: **no evidence that edge is anti-correlated with capacity, and explicitly NOT proof of absence.** **THE TWO STATISTICS DISAGREE IN SIGN** — the decile difference is POSITIVE while rho is NEGATIVE, which no monotone relationship can produce, so the decile split is not a clean size effect. **THE RESULT'S REAL CONTENT IS A CONTAMINATION MEASUREMENT**: a median apparent edge of 24.5¢ across all markets is implausible on its face — two legs costing 75.5¢ for a $1 payoff, a 32% riskless return on supposedly identical contracts — and the pre-committed trim drops 494→193 markets, i.e. **61% carry |edge|>20¢**, which `screen.py:26` already names as the signature of pairs that are NOT the same question. So the row is largely UNINFORMATIVE about its own hypothesis: the variable it correlates against is dominated by false pairs. **What it bought**: the hazard is unconfirmed but not excluded; a quantified 61% contamination indicator; and the conversion of the pre-registered largest threat into the critical path — **`match/gold/nonsports.csv` (144 blind pairs, key withheld) is now the binding constraint on every cross-venue statistic**. **Must not be concluded**: that any edge exists or is harvestable (no settlement read), or that the +19.3¢ decile difference is a finding. Re-running on a high-precision population requires a NEW row per rule 6; these criteria are immutable. L24 capture unaffected. | `research/exec/2026-08-20_capacity-edge-correlation/verdict.md` |

| L26 | 2026-09-02 | sports / settlement | **PENDING — MLB settlement-wedge rates (descriptive).** Registered BEFORE the run. `nodes/settle_wedge` classifies the Kalshi KXMLBGAME / Polymarket MLB game pair as BOUNDED on two dimensions: a postponement not completed within two days (Kalshi resolves to a fair price, Polymarket waits for the result) and a cancellation (Kalshi fair price, Polymarket 50-50). A BOUNDED dimension is priced as probability × worst-case loss, and the probability must come from a registered measurement or the dimension is UNKNOWN and every MLB cover blocks. This row measures those probabilities from the MLB Stats API schedule (2026 regular season through the registration date), counting every game exactly once in one state; `postponed_gt2d` is an UPPER BOUND wherever the reschedule date is null. ESPN cannot serve: it drops postponed games from their original date (probed 2026-04-10..23, 189 events all STATUS_FINAL). **Claims nothing about edge**; it prices a reserve. | L28 | 2026-09-03 | sports / coverage | **PENDING — co-listing census (descriptive, daily for seven days).** Registered BEFORE the first run. The operator's scope is every competition and every live event both venues list; sports were chosen because contract life is short and arbitrage may appear, which is a property of a market, not of a sport. This row measures it: both venues enumerate themselves (Kalshi `/series?category=Sports` then the open markets of every game-class series; Polymarket `/sports` — its own league table with settlement source and tag — then each tag's open events with a `moneyline` market carrying `gameStartTime`), the two are joined per event on the calendar date and both participants, and every competition is scored by co-listed capacity at the touch divided by contract life. The join is a counting device with declared looseness: the unmatched remainder is reported per competition and a competition whose unmatched count exceeds its co-listed count is labelled JOIN_WEAK. Seven daily runs; rank from the seven-day median; fewer than four runs orders nothing. The Kalshi ↔ Polymarket competition mapping is LEARNED from the join, never hand-seeded. **Reads no outcome, claims no edge**; it decides the order of the per-competition unblock workflow (citation → harvested templates → bounded rates). Probed while writing the spec (public, read-only, 2026-09-03): Kalshi 3,643 sports series, 262 `*GAME`; Polymarket 463 leagues in `/sports`; three-way soccer on Polymarket is three Yes/No moneyline markets per event (team, draw, team). | `research/sports/2026-09-03_co-listing-census/spec.yaml` |

Cells are (league, quantity, phase, flapping | clean): `end` is the last 600 s before the game's `post` transition (or its last row when the slate reaped it first) because today's only sub-par prints were closing books; `flapping` marks rows inside a socket_health interval where a venue was disconnected or its error count rose, or inside the declared 2026-09-03 IllegalTransition loop windows (16:54–18:50Z, 22:50–23:26Z) — kept and reported apart, never dropped. Blocked reasons keep the raw fault tail (`venue:FAULT`) so an unknown fault cannot hide behind BLOCKED_STALE. Every PROFITABLE_UNEXECUTED row is audited (phase, flapping, a stale-family fault on the same game within 60 s) and labelled SUSPECT or clean; labels, never deletes. **n for the forward paper row (GATE step 7), declared now**: over in-play clean profitable rows, n = max(30, ceil((1.96·σ(locked return)/0.005)²)); with fewer than 5 such rows, n = 30 by declaration. Rows outside the OpportunityStatus vocabulary (the retired REST lane's) are excluded by definition and counted. Reads no outcome; claims no edge: a profitable row is a print on the replicas the lane held, not a fill. | `research/sports/2026-09-04_cover-cost-distribution/spec.yaml` |

## Closures — 2026-09-02

Appended under rule 6 (*"Correct a row with a new row or an appended note, never in place"*). No
row above is edited, no criterion is changed, and no result is read. These record that forward
accrual **stopped**; they do not report an outcome, and none of them spends a test look.

The project narrowed to executable two-leg pure arbitrage on sports across independent venues.
The rows below belong to lanes that no longer serve that objective, or that had already stopped
accruing without anyone noticing.

| Row | Arm | Accrual window | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| **F1** | Kalshi-open vs PM established price (MLB moneyline) | 2026-08-20T22:04:58Z → 2026-09-02 | **CLOSED, unscored.** `com.janus.shadow` unloaded 2026-09-02; `collectors/collect_shadow.py` removed. Data preserved at `/Volumes/Kilos/janus/shadow/kalshi_open.sqlite` (WAL checkpointed, 4.1 MB merged before shutdown). **No outcome was read and none is claimed.** |
| **F2** | Kalshi↔PM convergence attribution (MLB moneyline) | 2026-08-21 → **2026-08-27 (de facto)** | **CLOSED, unscored.** Accrual in fact ended 2026-08-27: `com.janus.convergence` had been crash-looping every 30 s since 2026-08-20 on a `TypeError` in `game_key`, and its last write to `convergence/track.sqlite` was 2026-08-27T09:10. The failure was invisible for 13 days (15,207 identical tracebacks, 11.9 MB unrotated). Job unloaded 2026-09-02, collector removed, log archived gzipped under `logs/archive/`. **F2 is additionally superseded by scope**: it registers a *one-legged convergence* bet, and the current objective is explicitly two-leg pure arbitrage. **No outcome was read.** |
| **L24** | Newborn Kalshi/PM market + book capture | 2026-08-20 → 2026-09-02 | **CLOSED, capture retained.** `com.janus.newborn` unloaded; collector removed. ~1.4 M book rows with raw ladders archived intact at `/Volumes/Kilos/janus/archive/newborn_L24_closed_2026-09-02/` (WAL checkpointed, 21.6 MB merged). Cross-venue crypto/PM, not sports. Kept as the **shape reference** for the sports capture store — reuse its schema, never its contents as a sports result. |

Resuming any of these requires a **new row and a new forward clock**. The old clocks are spent:
a forward eval whose collector stopped cannot be silently continued later, because the gap is
indistinguishable from a period of no signal.

**Method note, recorded because it changes how future forward rows must be run.** F2 shows that a
registered forward evaluation can accrue nothing for six days while its row still reads `PENDING`.
Pre-registration guarantees the *criteria* are honest; it guarantees nothing about whether the
data is arriving. Forward rows from here carry an SLA in `ops/watchdog.py`, whose alerts are
demonstrated firing against a fake target per `GATE_TO_LIVE.md` step 2.

| | | | **[APPENDED 2026-09-12 — L85 corrected before deployment; four defects found by an independent audit of the same-day code.]** (a) **The clearance TTL.** `clearance_ttl_ms` is 500 ms from the mint on the loop, and L85 added a SECOND synchronous REST read after L77's (measured 187.7 ms). Sequential reads would routinely spend the window, the send would return CLEARANCE_SPENT, and the grid would back the whole game off as a venue rejection — the check meant to stop bad entries would have stopped every entry. The two reads are independent and now run concurrently (`max(a,b)`, not `a+b`), with `confirm_budget_ms` recording what was left so the margin stays measured. (b) **`at_mono` had no lower bound**: it is a monotonic stamp persisted into `protect_json`, so `protect_existing` can read one written before a reboot, when the difference is NEGATIVE and passed `<= entry_book_trust_s`. `maker.py:258` already rejects a negative book age for the same reason. (c) **`entry_book_trust_s` was never parsed** from `limits.toml`, so its documented "0 disables" lever did not exist. (d) **The skip used the full planned hedge size**: a padded FAK filling partially is the expected case, and the book may cover what is left at the walked price even when it could not cover the whole plan. All four pinned by tests that fail against the old condition. | research/exec/2026-09-12_hedge-leg-book-confirmation/spec.yaml |

The exhaustiveness check cannot catch this: `proof.outcome_ids` is minted from the same offers keys it is checked against (`poller.py:148`, `live_sports/grid.py:214`), so it compares labels this code path invented and is blind to a wrong instrument behind a right label. `MIN_CREDIBLE_GROSS = 0.70` is a 43 % locked return; the richest cover this lane ever settled was planned at 16.7 % (gross ~0.83, `0dfc6df3`, paid +$0.38), so every real edge on record passes with better than twice the headroom. **This is arithmetic standing in for an identity check; the identity fix is still owed.** | research/exec/2026-09-12_evaluation-sanity-floor/spec.yaml |

The board shows `age_s 0.0`. A frozen book priced against a live one manufactures the whole edge, which is what `nodes/book_replica`'s docstring exists to prevent. **Live in production**: the box runs without `--capture-series`, whose argparse default is `KXATPMATCH,KXWTAMATCH,KXATPCHALLENGERMATCH,KXWTACHALLENGERMATCH` plus PM tag `tennis` — exactly the leagues carrying the losses (atp 5 of 5 adverse, −$3.29). `unregister` now takes the owner's replica and drops only that registration; the last owner out still clears the book. That direction is the worse one. A leg bound to the wrong entity is not a mis-labelled leg, it is a **different instrument**: a soccer cover of Kalshi YES `B` + PM NO `A` pays $2 if B wins, $1 on the draw and $0 if A wins — a naked directional bet costing `p_B + (1 − p_A)`, which on a lopsided game prices like a 40–50 % edge, and the audit verified that shape survives the sizing filters with `mls` funded. The night of 2026-09-12, after the 23:36Z clear: **42 attempts, 3 fills**; 26 `BOOK_MOVED` (25 first-leg), and 6 `ORDER_REJECTED` all `PRECISION` — 5 contracts on a low-priced outcome falls under Polymarket's $1 notional minimum, an artifact of the measurement caps. The coordinator now solves the largest whole size at or under the plan's that both books support, applying per size exactly the tests the entry already applies: leg A's worst level at or under the limit, the hedge's AVERAGE at or under break-even, the all-in inside the plan's ceiling, the $1 minimum, and the registered dollar floor — which is the binding one on the way down, because profit is size-linear. Fees are not re-priced (that needs the offers, which live on the loop); the Kalshi per-order ceil is bounded by `CEIL_CENTS_USD / n` instead, so the check is conservative by construction. **One-way by design:** shrinking is legal because the reservation and every cap were cleared against the larger size; **growing is not**, because nothing re-checks a cap between the clearance and the wire — `gate.admit` binds only the opportunity id. | L96 | 2026-09-13 | execution/entry | **PENDING — every capped five-contract Polymarket order was killed by its own one-cent pad, 7 of 7, and the fix is to send at the venue's price, not to refuse.** `_cent_exact_buy` enforces TWO floors and only the dollar one was known above the adapter: the $1.00 maker notional, checked at cover time, and an unconditional floor on nominal SHARES — `size_d < 5.00`, re-checked as `n < 500` after the divisibility snap — that nothing upstream had ever heard of. It binds through L83: the order's collateral is capped at `q × the venue's best ask` and the adapter divides that ceiling by the price it SENDS (`wire = cap / price`), while the plan pads leg A one tick above top — so the wire always sees fewer than `q` shares. At five contracts that is **4.84 at top $0.30 and 4.948 at $0.95: under the floor at every price**. **The box's own rows settle it.** Of 216 executions since the 08:03Z release, the q=5 orders that carried a collateral ceiling produced **7 `PRECISION` and 1 `BOOK_MOVED` — zero fills, 100%** — and all seven had `marginal == top` with the full five contracts resting AT the top level: executable orders destroyed by their own pad. **L95's row blamed the measurement caps; that was the wrong mechanism and is corrected here.** The two q=5 covers that did fill came from the rows where the pre-send read failed, so no ceiling was applied at all — the unbounded-fill path L83 exists to close, and the same class as the 32.14-against-25 overfill that latched the kill. So the answer is not a refusal (a first cut of this row refused them, and the box's rows are what showed that would forfeit real fills): when a pad would shrink the wire under the floor, leg A is **repriced to the venue's own ask**, where the ceiling shrinks nothing and the nominal size is exactly `q`. Paying LESS than the limit cannot break economics that were cleared against paying MORE, so the direction needs no re-check; a book holding less than `q` at top gives a partial, which is the ordinary handled outcome; and if the cent-rounded ceiling cannot buy the whole size (possible only on a sub-cent book price) the order is left alone rather than the ceiling loosened. The sizer and L95's downsize keep the floor that is always true — a Polymarket leg under five contracts is unsendable at any price, and four contracts at $0.30 clears the $1 notional the cover already applies — refused as `VENUE_WIRE_MIN`. | research/exec/2026-09-13_wire-share-floor/spec.yaml |

On the reproduction (kalshi 0.40/0.45 against polymarket 0.60/0.62, q=100) the row read **gross 0.85 and −11.53c — eleven cents PAST actionable, with a locked return of 13.1% and $11.59 of net profit, on a row the lane BLOCKED**; the two-venue re-price of the same books reads **gross 1.02 and +5.35c, five cents SHORT**. Proven behaviour-neutral on the registered **MORIYA** schedule (L75's own case): same two attempts, same phases, same limits, same fill at 0.04, same end state — `hedge_book_reads` 1 instead of 2. So the maker closes only with a very short rest, and a short rest is exactly where fills were zero: of 17 post-only orders ever sent the **only** one that filled rested **20.46 s**. **Short rest, no fills; long rest, no economics.** And the faster the taker gets the worse the maker looks, because the maker's cost is incremental to a shrinking baseline — they are substitutes, and speed is the safer one (no GATE row, no F20 conflict, no fill-rate assumption). **Lower bound**: this is book drift; realised `hedge_slippage_c` also contains the chase (median 3.0c vs 0.78c here), and post-L85 realised p50 0.50c is the consistency check. It is also bimodal by outcome, not continuous: `RECONCILED` (n=10) and `ORDER_REJECTED` (n=7) show gap **0.0c at min, median and max**, while `BOOK_MOVED` (n=161) shows median **+7.0c**, p90 +13c, max +51c — every row that reached a fill saw the book not move at all. `PHANTOM_DEPTH_DOMINATES` failed outright: **0 of 161** declines held the planned price with short depth. | | | | **[APPENDED 2026-09-17 — L146 RESULT: `DEFECT_REPRODUCED`; BOTH CONTROLS CAUGHT THEIRS.]** The tested modules (`nodes/stream_polymarket`, `nodes/book_replica`, `nodes/stream_kalshi`, `nodes/execute/plan.py`, `kernel/schema.py`) were re-checked at verdict time: byte-identical to deployed `59dd5ae`, nothing uncommitted under `nodes/` or `kernel/`. Real objects throughout; only the Reconciler's REST fetch substituted. **Controls:** C1, the same lost update **at** the touch, was caught at once (drift 1, `RESYNCING`, `HASH_MISMATCH`, resnapshot requested); C2, a dropped Kalshi delta, was caught (`gap`, `SEQ_GAP`). **R1:** a book holding an ask the venue had removed received 60 agreeing updates — **60 checks, 0 drift, no fault on any, never selected by `due()`**, re-verified on every message while carrying a level that did not exist. **R2:** a local touch size of 8 against a venue touch of 1 — `agrees_with` returned `True` for both; the replica kept 8. **R3:** a quiet book was reconciled exactly as designed (adopted REST, `source="rest"`, `HASH_MISMATCH`, `rest_drift`=1), but `ps.books` still held the phantom level and **one agreeing update later the phantom was back, relabelled `"ws"`, with no fault**. A supporting pin isolates the cause: the same book over the same 20 s is selected for reconciliation when quiet and **never** when it carries agreeing traffic below the touch. On the constructed ladders the phantom makes 25 contracts look 0.28c cheaper for a buyer — **direction only, never a production estimate**. The exclusion of busy books is deliberate and its reason sound (`reconcile.py:9-11`, timing skew would read as drift); the defect is the gap between that filter and the stream's stated reliance on REST as the depth backstop (`protocol.py:18-20`). **Proves** the deployed code can carry wrong depth or touch size on an active book indefinitely while reporting it verified, recoverable only by a full websocket `book` event. **Does not prove** that it occurs in production, how often, or that it explains L143's losses — the below-touch loss rate is unmeasured. Candidate fixes for a separately registered row: make REST corrections write through to `ps.books` (smallest, no new false-drift exposure); stop a below-touch update from verifying the whole book; depth-check active books with a round-trip tolerance. All change `nodes/`; the latter two are entry-adjacent; each needs a spec, PENDING row, dated GATE amendment and operator release. Pinned by 10 tests that assert the defect so a fix flips them. No change to `nodes/`, `grids/`, `kernel/` or `ops/`; no box contact. | research/exec/2026-09-17_pm-depth-verification/verdict.md |

| | | | **[APPENDED 2026-09-17 — L147 RESULT: `SURFACE_INSUFFICIENT_FOR_20` AS REGISTERED, BUT NOT ROBUST; THE CAP AND CONVERSION BIND, NOT THE SURFACE.]** All 13 L28 census runs on the box (2026-09-03 → 2026-09-16, none partial; the census still runs daily, last success 2026-09-16 18:06 UTC). Median two-sided co-listed events **28.2/day** (window × 24/51) < 72, so the registered verdict is `SURFACE_INSUFFICIENT_FOR_20`, reported unchanged. **It rests on an artifact:** `two_sided_both` can only count events whose Polymarket book the census sampled, and in every run it was 79–98% of books sampled (median 92%), tracking that count at Pearson r = 0.94 across runs (88 sampled → 86 two-sided; 59 → 56). The field was chosen from the census spec's definition before its population method was checked — the third registered statistic in four rows that was right in intent and wrong in how it is populated (after L143's pooled rho and L144's favourable fraction). What the data supports: median **116 co-listed events/day** (range 101–309), and ~92% of sampled events were two-sided, suggesting ~107 two-sided/day — **above** 72, an estimate assuming the round-robin sample is representative. So the surface is probably **not** the binding limit. What binds: **(1)** the $500/day notional cap, under which filled covers count after settling, allows ~24 covers → **~$6.67/day** at the $0.28 best case; **(2)** conversion — $20/day needs 0.61 profitable covers per co-listed event against ~0.08 observed (~8×), and $50/day needs 1.5 per event, more than one on every event daily; **(3)** depth — median touch notional per competition is $3–$33 on Kalshi and $5–$17 on Polymarket against ~$21 committed per cover, so covers walk down the book where L146 showed the Polymarket replica can hold phantom levels. $0.28 is a best case throughout (the L141 window netted −$0.16 over four covers). A co-listed event is necessary, not sufficient. Read-only. | research/sports/2026-09-17_surface-for-20-a-day/verdict.md |
| | | | **[APPENDED 2026-09-17 — L148 RESULT: `FAILED`; THE REST WRITE-THROUGH IS WITHDRAWN AND REVERTED.]** Implemented, tested and put through two adversarial review rounds; nothing deployed; `nodes/` is back to HEAD. The attempted code and both reports are kept at `attempted_write_through.patch`, `review_round1.json`, `review_round2.json`. **Round 1** found four defects the first version introduced, each confirmed by two skeptics driving the real classes: **WT-1/B1/F2 (major)** — a second owner of the same token that never received a `book` stays unsynced, is never superseded and always mismatches, so newer websocket depth was overwritten; this is the production shape (capture watchlist + game poller); **WT-2** — a replica invalidated for HEARTBEAT_LOST during the flight, likewise; **M1** — a malformed `book` after a drift recreates an empty ladder, defeating a presence check; **F1** — REST ahead of queued frames causes fail-closed false drift. A corrected version (no registration superseded, disagreement from a synced replica, stream ladder equal to what it held) closed WT-1/WT-2/M1, with regression tests shown to fail on the first version. **Round 2**: the in-process guard held and the fix worked in every realistic shape, but two reviewers independently found, and both skeptics reproduced, **N1/WT-3**: the guard proves the stream's ladder did not change during the flight but cannot prove REST is newer than the websocket state already in it, so a lagging REST response makes a level the websocket already removed permanent — relabelled `"ws"`, re-verified by agreeing traffic, never re-checked. Before the change it lasted one message. That falsifies the registered criterion ("finds no way … to overwrite newer websocket state"). The trigger needs REST to lag the websocket by ~15 s, unmeasured, but Polymarket's own client calls a throttled response "a stale quote wearing a fresh wall-clock timestamp" and REST has no sequence number. Round 2 also found (**T1**) that removing any single guard left all 18 tests green. Any write-through must decide which source is newer and Polymarket gives no way to; the approach is withdrawn in favour of L149. | research/exec/2026-09-17_rest-correction-write-through/verdict.md |

The independent review found **no sequence** leaving a stale level once the resnapshot is answered, over ~2,600 simulated runs of the real classes. It recorded two **minor, conditional costs this change introduces**: (1) a quiet book on which REST persistently disagrees now alternates between the socket's and REST's book about every 20 s instead of settling on REST's, re-arming the single-flight guard because the source label is in the sizing hash, at ~3 resubscribes/min per such asset (frequency unknown: `rest_drift` is unpersisted); (2) if the venue ignored the re-subscribe after applying the unsubscribe, the asset would be served on REST content without the 3 s bound biting — one real capture showed 300/305 drift resubscribes answered by a book (median 0.23 s). It also surfaced **pre-existing** defects, not caused or fixed here: **major, fail-closed** — a discovery refresh that changes a game's executable spec unregisters and re-registers the same Polymarket token in one tick, so no frame is sent, the ladder is dropped and the new replica stays DISCONNECTED until a reconnect (and the capture watchlist's registration is dropped), which may cost entry rate; minor — after a reconnect a REST read older than a book already received can be adopted; note — a half-applied malformed book can keep stale asks. Fixes R3 on quiet books only; R1/R2 remain. Not a profitability lever. Changes `nodes/`, so deploying needs the operator's release, and it must not be deployed during F24. | research/exec/2026-09-17_rest-mismatch-resnapshot/verdict.md |

| | | | **[APPENDED 2026-09-17 — F24 PREPARATION AUDITED; FOUR BLOCKERS FOUND AND CORRECTED; ARMING BLOCKED BY A NEW ORPHAN_FILL.]** An independent adversarial audit (65 agents, 30 findings, 17 surviving verification) of `arm_f24.sh`, `check_f24.sh`, `report.py` and the paperwork found **four blockers**. (1) `ops/box/run.sh` stops polling after its own budget while the remote command runs on, so a slow, interrupted or Ctrl-C'd arm could leave the box armed and trading while the local script exited non-zero printing nothing — and the runbook told the operator that meant nothing had changed. (2-4) **The "$50 cumulative loss" and "30 covers" ends are enforced by nothing**: they exist only as fields in a report a human runs. The only automatic bounds are the **$25 per-UTC-day realised-loss breaker** and the override expiry, and the breaker's bucket resets at midnight, so seven days at -$24 latch nothing: the true unattended worst case is **~$175, not ~$50**. The operator was told this and chose the full 7-day window with daily checks (the $50 stop is therefore human-enforced, by reading the report at least daily). Corrections, all local and untested against the box except as noted: `arm_f24.sh` gains a remote lock, a refusal if a start pin already exists, a copy of the F22 override into the audit directory before it is overwritten, a second quiet check after the wait, an idempotent F22-disabled rule (two report ticks plus the log's last marker, because `grid.check_arm` prints it only on change), a start pin at `/srv/janus/release-audit/f24_start_utc`, and — the blocker fix — it now ALWAYS ends by running the new read-only **`verify_f24.sh`** and exits with the box's verified state (ARMED / NOT_ARMED / INDETERMINATE) rather than the send's. `report.py` was rewritten for seven defects: PARTIAL and UNWOUND settlements were scored as covers (UNWOUND was missed by the audit and found during the fix); no stop condition was ever detected; the $50 end returned STOP instead of LOSES_MONEY; `--include/--exclude` were invisible in the output; PAPER_EXECUTION rows could be counted as real money; a $/day figure was extrapolated from a partial day, which the spec prohibits; and a mistyped start_utc could produce a final verdict over pre-F24 rows. Tests: 37 in `test_report.py` (from 10) and 26 in `test_arm_f24.py` (from 14), each new one shown to fail without its fix; full suite 1,778. **Calibration re-run on the box (read-only) reproduces L141 exactly under the stricter cover predicate: 4 settled covers, -$0.16449, `released_settlement_rows` 4 = `settled_covers` 4, nothing excluded.** RUNBOOK.md now states which bounds are automatic and which depend on the daily read. **Arming is blocked:** a new `ORPHAN_FILL` was raised at 2026-09-17T19:36:19Z (Kalshi `KXTRUMPSAY-26SEP...` buy_yes 6.67 @ 0.68), 31 minutes after the 19:05Z pre-flight passed. The operator confirms it is their own manual trade and that they may keep trading manually during F24. `arm_f24.sh` refuses while it is unresolved, as designed; the kill is unchanged. Note for the row: a manual fill during F24 latches `kill/live` (`nodes/execute/guard.py`), which is a registered STOP condition and ends F24. | research/sports/2026-09-17_limited-live-f24-taker-profitability/RUNBOOK.md, verify_f24.sh |
| | | | **[APPENDED 2026-09-18 — F24 TOOLING, AUDIT ROUNDS 3-5: STOP-SIGNAL BLINDNESS, THEN A CHANGE OF METHOD.]** A third adversarial audit found three blockers of one family: the tooling asked "can the box trade?" by ENUMERATING stop causes, and the deployed code always had one more that leaves no durable trace. Verified in `59dd5ae`: the kill directory holds **five** scopes (`all`, `live`, `venue-*`, `event-*`, `strategy-*`), not the two that were checked; `LossBreaker.latch` writes `kill/breaker.json` **and** `kill/live`, after which `evaluate()` returns BREAKER_LATCHED from the file BEFORE any kill check, so clearing `kill/live` does NOT resume trading and the kill is never re-created — and **nothing under `ops/` calls `LossBreaker.reset`**, so a latch is a human decision, not a file to delete; and `breaker.evaluate()` also returns PNL_UNKNOWN while one fill is UNCONFIRMED, refusing every candidate for the rest of the UTC day while writing nothing anywhere. Round 4 fixed those; a prover then found four more of the same shape, including two OPPOSITE errors in the new day-classification logic (one cleared candidate whitewashing a day with KILL_SWITCH x241,500; a single n=1 tick STOPping a row with the false line "EVERY candidate was refused"). **Round 5 changed method instead of adding another cause.** The lane already RECORDS why it refused, per report tick, in `refusal_counts(seen_ts, channel, code, league, n)` in the same database the reader opens read-only — live on the box: 26,947 rows, and with the kill latched the risk channel reads KILL_SWITCH 6084 against IN_FLIGHT 4111. Both tools now judge from that observed record, and `report.py` was made SUBTRACTIVE: the day taxonomy (ATTEMPTED/BLOCKED/QUIET) and every STOP derived from counters are gone; it reports numbers per completed UTC day and WITHHOLDS the verdict (`NEEDS_HUMAN_LOOK`) where the counts are consistent with the lane having been unable to trade, because a wrong withholding costs a glance and a wrong claim costs the answer. Only durable signals may END a row. **Four corrections made against the real box, each of which would have broken the row:** (1) the kill directory holds `.guard-failure-*.lock` and `.legacy-incident-*.lock`, which `kill.active()` can never match — failing closed on them would have made arming permanently impossible, so they are reported and ignored; (2) the socket rule required ANY venue up, but a cover is two legs, so it now requires both; (3) `report.py` outgrew SSM's 97 KB command limit (113,875 bytes) and `check_f24.sh` could not run at all — the reader now travels gzip+base64 and the report comes back compressed, with the banner rendered locally, because the 24 KB stdout cap had been truncating the authoritative last line; (4) **the lane's loop stalls**: measured 2026-09-18 at ~1.4 s with spikes past its 5 s bound, and the pre-kill record shows **CLOCK_UNHEALTHY n=1546 over 122 ticks in one 8-hour stretch**, so gating the verdict on the instantaneous stall flag would make ARMED unreachable — instantaneous health is now evidence, and a stall that is really refusing candidates is decided by the observed record, where CLOCK_UNHEALTHY and VENUE_DISCONNECTED are blocking. Tests: `test_arm_f24.py` 92, `test_report.py` 111, 203 in the directory, full suite 1,778; the arming tests EXTRACT AND RUN the embedded confirmation and probe against fake box state. Verified live, read-only: `verify_f24.sh` answers **NOT_ARMED** on the real box (F22 kill present and byte-exact, F22 override untouched, both sockets up, lock files correctly disregarded), and `check_f24.sh` reproduces L141 through the new transport: 4 covers, all-in -$0.16449 identical to covers-only, $49.84 of further loss from the $50 end. **NOT ARMED**; an unresolved ORPHAN_FILL (the operator's own manual Kalshi trade) and a GUARD_FAILED block the quiet check, as designed. | research/sports/2026-09-17_limited-live-f24-taker-profitability/RUNBOOK.md |
